===== D:\Android\sim_sales_manager\lib\core\notifications\notification_id.dart =====
/// `flutter_local_notifications` schedules/cancels by a plain 32-bit
/// int id, not a string — this derives a stable one from our own
/// string row id (a uuid) so a later cancel can recompute the same int
/// without needing a separate lookup table. `& 0x7fffffff` keeps it a
/// positive 32-bit value (Android's notification id is a Java `int`).
int notificationOsId(String rowId) => rowId.hashCode & 0x7fffffff;
===== D:\Android\sim_sales_manager\lib\core\notifications\notification_scheduler.dart =====
import 'package:flutter_local_notifications/flutter_local_notifications.dart';
import 'package:timezone/data/latest_all.dart' as tz_data;
import 'package:timezone/timezone.dart' as tz;

/// Thin wrapper around `flutter_local_notifications` — the one place
/// that talks to the OS notification/alarm system directly.
/// `NotificationsCubit` is the only caller; it owns the actual
/// scheduling *decisions* (what to schedule, when to cancel), this just
/// executes them.
class NotificationScheduler {
  final FlutterLocalNotificationsPlugin _plugin = FlutterLocalNotificationsPlugin();
  bool _initialized = false;

  static const _channelId = 'esim_dafter_reminders';
  static const _channelName = 'Reminders';
  static const _channelDescription = 'Line expiry, customer debt, and company balance reminders';

  Future<void> init() async {
    if (_initialized) return;
    // Only used so `zonedSchedule` below has a loaded timezone database to
    // work with at all — this app deliberately never resolves the
    // device's actual named zone (`tz.local` defaults to, and stays,
    // UTC with nothing more done here; there's no bundled/platform-
    // channel way to ask for the real one without a new package). Every
    // `DateTime` this app schedules against is converted straight to a
    // UTC instant before it ever reaches `TZDateTime` (see [scheduleAt])
    // — Dart's own `DateTime.toUtc()` already correctly accounts for the
    // device's real offset, so the absolute moment scheduled is correct
    // regardless of what "local" means to the `timezone` package itself.
    tz_data.initializeTimeZones();

    const androidSettings = AndroidInitializationSettings('@mipmap/ic_launcher');
    const iosSettings = DarwinInitializationSettings();
    await _plugin.initialize(
      const InitializationSettings(android: androidSettings, iOS: iosSettings),
    );

    final androidPlugin = _plugin.resolvePlatformSpecificImplementation<AndroidFlutterLocalNotificationsPlugin>();
    await androidPlugin?.createNotificationChannel(
      const AndroidNotificationChannel(_channelId, _channelName, description: _channelDescription),
    );

    _initialized = true;
  }

  Future<bool> requestPermission() async {
    final androidPlugin = _plugin.resolvePlatformSpecificImplementation<AndroidFlutterLocalNotificationsPlugin>();
    if (androidPlugin != null) {
      return await androidPlugin.requestNotificationsPermission() ?? false;
    }
    final iosPlugin = _plugin.resolvePlatformSpecificImplementation<IOSFlutterLocalNotificationsPlugin>();
    if (iosPlugin != null) {
      return await iosPlugin.requestPermissions(alert: true, badge: true, sound: true) ?? false;
    }
    return true;
  }

  /// Whether Android currently actually lets this app show notifications —
  /// distinct from [requestPermission]'s one-shot result at cold start:
  /// Android only shows that OS prompt once, ever, so a seller who denied
  /// it (or later revoked it from Settings) never gets asked again, and
  /// `requestNotificationsPermission()` just silently returns `false` from
  /// then on with no further UI. This is what actually explains "the
  /// reminder never showed while the app was closed" even though the row
  /// is saved correctly and its OS alarm still fires on schedule — the
  /// alarm fires, `AndroidScheduleMode` still runs, but Android drops the
  /// notification itself right before display because permission isn't
  /// granted. `NotificationsPage` polls this to show a real, actionable
  /// warning instead of a reminder that looks scheduled but can never
  /// actually be seen. `true` on iOS/non-Android, where this isn't
  /// resolvable the same way and isn't this app's primary target anyway.
  Future<bool> areNotificationsEnabled() async {
    final androidPlugin = _plugin.resolvePlatformSpecificImplementation<AndroidFlutterLocalNotificationsPlugin>();
    if (androidPlugin == null) return true;
    return await androidPlugin.areNotificationsEnabled() ?? false;
  }

  NotificationDetails get _details => const NotificationDetails(
        android: AndroidNotificationDetails(_channelId, _channelName, channelDescription: _channelDescription),
        iOS: DarwinNotificationDetails(),
      );

  /// Schedules [title]/[body] to appear at [at] (interpreted as whatever
  /// timezone [at] itself carries — local wall-clock time for every
  /// caller in this app; converted to UTC below before being wrapped in
  /// a `TZDateTime`, which is what actually determines the real fire
  /// instant, not the `Location` tag it carries). A past [at] fires
  /// almost immediately instead (Android/iOS both handle this
  /// gracefully) — callers that need "never schedule for the past"
  /// filter before calling this, not here.
  Future<void> scheduleAt({required int id, required String title, required String body, required DateTime at}) async {
    await _plugin.zonedSchedule(
      id,
      title,
      body,
      tz.TZDateTime.from(at.toUtc(), tz.UTC),
      _details,
      androidScheduleMode: AndroidScheduleMode.inexactAllowWhileIdle,
      uiLocalNotificationDateInterpretation: UILocalNotificationDateInterpretation.absoluteTime,
    );
  }

  Future<void> showNow({required int id, required String title, required String body}) async {
    await _plugin.show(id, title, body, _details);
  }

  Future<void> cancel(int id) => _plugin.cancel(id);
}
===== D:\Android\sim_sales_manager\lib\core\notifications\notification_settings_bridge.dart =====
import 'package:flutter/services.dart';

/// Native-settings bridge (see `MainActivity.kt`) — the notifications page
/// uses this to send the seller straight to this app's OS notification
/// settings, or its general app-info page, with one tap. There's no
/// Flutter-side API for either (notification permission, once denied,
/// can't be re-requested through the plugin — only the OS settings screen
/// itself can flip it back on).
class NotificationSettingsBridge {
  static const _channel = MethodChannel('esim_dafter/notification_settings');

  static Future<void> openNotificationSettings() async {
    try {
      await _channel.invokeMethod('openNotificationSettings');
    } catch (_) {
      // Best-effort — some OEMs/older Android versions may not resolve
      // this intent; openAppSettings is the fallback callers use.
    }
  }

  /// Fallback when `openNotificationSettings` isn't available (rare OEMs) —
  /// also where the seller can find their OEM's own "battery
  /// optimization"/"auto-start" toggle, which this app has no standard,
  /// Play-policy-safe API to jump straight to.
  static Future<void> openAppSettings() async {
    try {
      await _channel.invokeMethod('openAppSettings');
    } catch (_) {
      // Nothing more we can do — the OS itself refused to open it.
    }
  }
}
===== D:\Android\sim_sales_manager\lib\core\notifications\notification_type.dart =====
/// What a notification is about — drives its icon/tap-target in the
/// inbox and, for a couple of types, whether a later event should
/// cancel it before it ever fires (see `NotificationsCubit`).
enum NotificationType {
  /// A line's `expiryDate` is 3 days away. [relatedEntityId] is the
  /// Line's id.
  lineExpiringSoon,

  /// A line's `expiryDate` is today. [relatedEntityId] is the Line's id.
  lineExpired,

  /// A seller-set "remind me" for a customer's outstanding balance.
  /// [relatedEntityId] is the Customer's id — cancelled automatically if
  /// the customer's total owed reaches zero before it fires.
  customerDebtReminder,

  /// A company's `availableToSell` dropped to zero or below.
  /// [relatedEntityId] is the Company's id.
  companyLowBalance,

  /// A shipment was recorded with an unpaid remainder — what's owed to
  /// that supplier. [relatedEntityId] is the Company's id.
  companyOwedAlert,

  /// Admin-only: a seller submitted a new Support & Feedback request.
  /// Raised live via `SupportRequestRealtimeGate`'s Supabase Realtime
  /// subscription, not scheduled ahead of time like every other type
  /// here — see its doc comment for why this only works while the admin
  /// app is open. [relatedEntityId] is unused (there's no per-request
  /// detail route to jump to yet, just the Support & Feedback list).
  newSupportRequest,
}

String notificationTypeToRow(NotificationType type) => type.name;

NotificationType? notificationTypeFromRow(String value) {
  for (final type in NotificationType.values) {
    if (type.name == value) return type;
  }
  return null;
}
===== D:\Android\sim_sales_manager\lib\core\notifications\notifications_cubit.dart =====
import 'dart:async';

import 'package:drift/drift.dart';
import 'package:easy_localization/easy_localization.dart';
import 'package:flutter_bloc/flutter_bloc.dart';

import '../auth/auth_service.dart';
import '../config/env.dart';
import '../database/app_database.dart';
import '../di/service_locator.dart';
import '../sync/sync_engine.dart';
import '../utils/uuid.dart';
import 'app_notification.dart';
import 'notification_id.dart';
import 'notification_scheduler.dart';
import 'notification_type.dart';
import 'notifications_state.dart';

/// Owns the local `notifications` table end to end: the in-app inbox
/// (`state.notifications`) and, in lockstep, whatever's actually armed
/// at the OS level through `NotificationScheduler`. Every feature that
/// needs to raise or clear a reminder goes through this Cubit's public
/// methods rather than touching `NotificationScheduler` directly, so
/// the inbox table and the real scheduled alarms can never drift apart.
///
/// **Line expiry** (`scheduleLineReminders`/`cancelLineReminders`):
/// called by `CustomersCubit` on every line add/edit/delete — 3 days
/// before `expiryDate` and again on the day itself, both at 9am local
/// time. **Customer debt** (`scheduleDebtReminder`/
/// `cancelDebtRemindersIfSettled`): a seller-picked 1-30 day "remind me"
/// per customer, auto-cancelled the moment that customer's total owed
/// reaches zero. **Company balance** (`raiseOrClearLowBalanceAlert`):
/// fires once when a company's `availableToSell` drops to zero or
/// below, clears itself the moment it recovers above zero so the next
/// dip can alert again. **Company owed** (`notifyCompanyOwed`): a
/// one-shot alert whenever a shipment is recorded with an unpaid
/// remainder — there's no "mark this shipment paid" flow to hook an
/// auto-cancel to (unlike a Line's `addPayment`), so unlike the other
/// three this one is fire-and-forget, not a persistent tracked state.
class NotificationsCubit extends Cubit<NotificationsState> {
  final AppDatabase? _db;
  final AuthService? _auth;
  final SyncEngine? _syncEngine;
  final NotificationScheduler _scheduler;

  NotificationsCubit({AppDatabase? db, AuthService? auth, SyncEngine? syncEngine, NotificationScheduler? scheduler})
    : _db = _resolveDb(db),
      _auth = _resolveAuth(auth),
      _syncEngine = syncEngine ?? (Env.isConfigured ? getIt<SyncEngine>() : null),
      _scheduler = scheduler ?? NotificationScheduler(),
      super(const NotificationsState()) {
    if (_db != null && _auth?.currentOwnerId != null) {
      unawaited(_init());
    }
  }

  static AppDatabase? _resolveDb(AppDatabase? db) => db ?? (Env.isConfigured ? getIt<AppDatabase>() : null);
  static AuthService? _resolveAuth(AuthService? auth) => auth ?? (Env.isConfigured ? getIt<AuthService>() : null);

  bool get _isReal => _db != null && _auth?.currentOwnerId != null;

  /// Whether Android will actually let a scheduled reminder be seen —
  /// see `NotificationScheduler.areNotificationsEnabled`'s doc comment.
  /// `NotificationsPage` checks this to warn the seller when it's `false`,
  /// since a "successfully scheduled" reminder that Android silently
  /// drops at display time looks identical to a working one from
  /// anywhere else in this app.
  Future<bool> notificationsEnabled() => _scheduler.areNotificationsEnabled();

  Future<void> _init() async {
    // Same isolation reasoning as _rearmAllPending below: neither of these
    // may ever be allowed to throw past this point and skip the rest of
    // _init, or the state reload at the very end (the actual source of
    // truth for the in-app inbox/debt-reminder-button state) would never
    // run at all.
    try {
      await _scheduler.init();
      await _scheduler.requestPermission();
    } catch (_) {
      // Scheduling/permission setup failed — still proceed so the local
      // database is at least correctly reflected in-app.
    }
    // Pulls back whatever survived on Supabase before anything else —
    // this is what lets a still-future reminder recover after the local
    // database was wiped (cleared app storage, reinstall) instead of
    // just silently vanishing. See notifications_table.dart's doc
    // comment.
    final engine = _syncEngine;
    final ownerId = _auth?.currentOwnerId;
    if (engine != null && ownerId != null) {
      try {
        await engine.pushPendingNotifications(ownerId);
        await engine.pullNotifications(ownerId);
      } catch (_) {
        // Offline or transient failure — proceed with local data.
      }
    }
    await _rearmAllPending();
    await _loadFromDrift();
  }

  Future<void> _pushBestEffort() async {
    final engine = _syncEngine;
    final ownerId = _auth?.currentOwnerId;
    if (engine == null || ownerId == null) return;
    try {
      await engine.pushPendingNotifications(ownerId);
    } catch (_) {
      // Offline or transient failure — next sync cycle retries.
    }
  }

  /// Re-schedules every still-future notification with the OS on every
  /// cold start — `zonedSchedule` calls for the same id just replace
  /// whatever was there, so this is safe to call unconditionally. Covers
  /// both a genuine device reboot (Android clears `AlarmManager` alarms
  /// then, the plugin's own boot receiver only catches what it had
  /// already persisted natively) and a fresh install/first run.
  ///
  /// This used to be the actual root cause of the inbox/debt-reminder
  /// button looking reset on every app restart: `_init()` calls this
  /// then `_loadFromDrift()` right after (see `_init()` above), and this
  /// loop called the real OS plugin with no try/catch — a single native
  /// scheduling failure (the same class of real-device-only failure
  /// `_insertAndSchedule` was fixed against) threw straight out of
  /// `_init()`'s `unawaited(...)` call, silently killing it before
  /// `_loadFromDrift()` ever ran. That left `state.notifications` empty
  /// for the entire app session — every cold start, regardless of what
  /// was actually saved locally — until some unrelated action happened
  /// to call `_loadFromDrift()` itself. Isolating each row's OS call
  /// like this guarantees `_init()` always reaches `_loadFromDrift()`,
  /// so the in-app state always reflects the database again.
  Future<void> _rearmAllPending() async {
    final db = _db!;
    final ownerId = _auth!.currentOwnerId!;
    final now = DateTime.now();
    final rows = await (db.select(db.notificationsTable)
          ..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.fireAt.isBiggerThanValue(now)))
        .get();
    for (final row in rows) {
      try {
        await _scheduler.scheduleAt(id: row.osNotificationId, title: row.title, body: row.body, at: row.fireAt);
      } catch (_) {
        // This one row's OS alarm didn't re-arm; every other row (and,
        // critically, the state reload right after this method returns)
        // must still proceed.
      }
    }
  }

  Future<void> _loadFromDrift() async {
    final db = _db!;
    final ownerId = _auth!.currentOwnerId!;
    final rows = await (db.select(db.notificationsTable)..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId))).get();
    final notifications = rows
        .map((row) {
          final type = notificationTypeFromRow(row.type);
          if (type == null) return null;
          return AppNotification(
            id: row.id,
            type: type,
            title: row.title,
            body: row.body,
            relatedEntityId: row.relatedEntityId,
            fireAt: row.fireAt,
            read: row.read,
          );
        })
        .whereType<AppNotification>()
        .toList();
    emit(state.copyWith(notifications: notifications));
  }

  void markRead(String id) {
    if (!_isReal) return;
    final db = _db!;
    unawaited((db.update(db.notificationsTable)..where((t) => t.id.equals(id)))
        .write(NotificationsTableCompanion(read: const Value(true), synced: const Value(false), updatedAt: Value(DateTime.now())))
        .then((_) => _pushBestEffort()));
    emit(state.copyWith(notifications: state.notifications.map((n) => n.id == id ? AppNotification(
          id: n.id,
          type: n.type,
          title: n.title,
          body: n.body,
          relatedEntityId: n.relatedEntityId,
          fireAt: n.fireAt,
          read: true,
        ) : n).toList()));
  }

  Future<void> _insertAndSchedule({
    required NotificationType type,
    required String title,
    required String body,
    required String? relatedEntityId,
    required DateTime fireAt,
  }) async {
    final db = _db!;
    final ownerId = _auth!.currentOwnerId!;
    final id = generateUuidV4();
    final osId = notificationOsId(id);
    await db.into(db.notificationsTable).insert(NotificationsTableCompanion.insert(
          id: id,
          ownerId: ownerId,
          osNotificationId: osId,
          type: notificationTypeToRow(type),
          title: title,
          body: body,
          relatedEntityId: Value(relatedEntityId),
          fireAt: fireAt,
          synced: const Value(false),
          updatedAt: DateTime.now(),
        ));
    // The Drift row is the source of truth (per this app's offline-first
    // rule) — refresh state from it right away, before touching the OS
    // alarm below. `flutter_local_notifications`'s real plugin can throw
    // on a real device in ways the tests' fake scheduler never does
    // (missing notification permission, exact-alarm restrictions, OEM
    // quirks) — if that call threw *before* this reload ran, the row
    // would already be saved but the UI would never find out, since
    // nothing here awaits/catches this method's Future. Best-effort and
    // isolated so a native scheduling failure can never again block the
    // in-app state from reflecting what's actually in the database.
    await _loadFromDrift();
    try {
      if (fireAt.isAfter(DateTime.now())) {
        await _scheduler.scheduleAt(id: osId, title: title, body: body, at: fireAt);
      } else {
        await _scheduler.showNow(id: osId, title: title, body: body);
      }
    } catch (_) {
      // The reminder still exists and shows in the inbox; only the OS-level
      // alarm/heads-up failed to arm. _rearmAllPending retries this on
      // every cold start.
    }
    unawaited(_pushBestEffort());
  }

  /// Cancels every notification matching [match]: removes its OS alarm,
  /// deletes the local row, and tombstones+pushes the delete to Supabase
  /// (same `recordAndPushDelete` mechanism `CompaniesCubit`/
  /// `CustomersCubit` use — see its doc comment) so a pull can never
  /// resurrect a reminder that was correctly cancelled here.
  Future<void> _cancelWhere(bool Function(NotificationsTableData) match) async {
    final db = _db!;
    final ownerId = _auth!.currentOwnerId!;
    final rows = await (db.select(db.notificationsTable)..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId))).get();
    for (final row in rows.where(match)) {
      // Same ordering fix as _insertAndSchedule: cancel the OS alarm
      // best-effort first, but never let a native-plugin failure there
      // stop the row from actually being deleted and reflected below.
      try {
        await _scheduler.cancel(row.osNotificationId);
      } catch (_) {
        // Worst case a stale alarm fires once more later; the row (the
        // source of truth) is still removed right after this.
      }
      await (db.delete(db.notificationsTable)..where((t) => t.id.equals(row.id))).go();
      unawaited(_syncEngine?.recordAndPushDelete(entityTable: 'notifications', id: row.id, ownerId: ownerId) ?? Future.value());
    }
    await _loadFromDrift();
  }

  // ── Line expiry ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  static DateTime _atNineAm(DateTime date) => DateTime(date.year, date.month, date.day, 9);

  /// Call on every line add/edit — clears any previous reminders for
  /// this line first, so an edited expiry date never leaves a stale
  /// alarm behind pointing at the old date.
  Future<void> scheduleLineReminders({
    required String lineId,
    required String simNumber,
    required String customerName,
    required DateTime expiryDate,
  }) async {
    if (!_isReal) return;
    await cancelLineReminders(lineId);

    final threeDaysBefore = _atNineAm(expiryDate.subtract(const Duration(days: 3)));
    if (threeDaysBefore.isAfter(DateTime.now())) {
      await _insertAndSchedule(
        type: NotificationType.lineExpiringSoon,
        title: 'notifications.lineExpiringSoon.title'.tr(),
        body: 'notifications.lineExpiringSoon.body'.tr(namedArgs: {'sim': simNumber, 'customer': customerName}),
        relatedEntityId: lineId,
        fireAt: threeDaysBefore,
      );
    }

    final onTheDay = _atNineAm(expiryDate);
    if (onTheDay.isAfter(DateTime.now())) {
      await _insertAndSchedule(
        type: NotificationType.lineExpired,
        title: 'notifications.lineExpired.title'.tr(),
        body: 'notifications.lineExpired.body'.tr(namedArgs: {'sim': simNumber, 'customer': customerName}),
        relatedEntityId: lineId,
        fireAt: onTheDay,
      );
    }
  }

  Future<void> cancelLineReminders(String lineId) async {
    if (!_isReal) return;
    await _cancelWhere((row) =>
        row.relatedEntityId == lineId &&
        (row.type == notificationTypeToRow(NotificationType.lineExpiringSoon) ||
            row.type == notificationTypeToRow(NotificationType.lineExpired)));
  }

  // ── Customer debt ───────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Future<void> scheduleDebtReminder({
    required String customerId,
    required String customerName,
    required double amountOwed,
    required int days,
  }) async {
    if (!_isReal) return;
    // Same "clear the old one first" pattern scheduleLineReminders uses —
    // without this, tapping "Schedule reminder" again for a customer who
    // already has one pending (or re-testing the feature repeatedly)
    // just stacks another row instead of replacing it, so the inbox
    // fills up with duplicate reminders for the same customer over time.
    await cancelDebtReminder(customerId);
    await _insertAndSchedule(
      type: NotificationType.customerDebtReminder,
      title: 'notifications.customerDebtReminder.title'.tr(),
      body: 'notifications.customerDebtReminder.body'.tr(namedArgs: {'customer': customerName, 'amount': amountOwed.toStringAsFixed(2)}),
      relatedEntityId: customerId,
      fireAt: DateTime.now().add(Duration(days: days)),
    );
  }

  /// Call after recording any payment against a customer's lines —
  /// cancels every pending debt reminder for [customerId] once
  /// [totalOwed] confirms nothing's left to collect.
  Future<void> cancelDebtRemindersIfSettled({required String customerId, required double totalOwed}) async {
    if (!_isReal || totalOwed > 0) return;
    await _cancelWhere((row) => row.relatedEntityId == customerId && row.type == notificationTypeToRow(NotificationType.customerDebtReminder));
  }

  /// Manual cancel from the customer detail page's reminder row — unlike
  /// [cancelDebtRemindersIfSettled] this isn't gated on the debt actually
  /// being paid off, since the seller may just want to call off a
  /// reminder they scheduled by mistake or too far out.
  Future<void> cancelDebtReminder(String customerId) async {
    if (!_isReal) return;
    await _cancelWhere((row) => row.relatedEntityId == customerId && row.type == notificationTypeToRow(NotificationType.customerDebtReminder));
  }

  /// The still-pending (future `fireAt`) debt reminder for [customerId],
  /// if any — lets the customer detail page show real persisted state
  /// instead of ephemeral widget state that resets on navigation.
  AppNotification? pendingDebtReminder(String customerId) {
    for (final n in state.notifications) {
      if (n.relatedEntityId == customerId && n.type == NotificationType.customerDebtReminder && n.isPending) return n;
    }
    return null;
  }

  // ── Company balance ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  /// Call after every balance recompute (`CompaniesCubit._loadFromDrift`)
  /// with the company's current `availableToSell` — raises a one-shot
  /// alert the moment it's at/below zero (skipped if one's already
  /// pending, so a dozen reloads in a row don't spam a dozen alerts),
  /// and clears that alert the moment the balance recovers above zero so
  /// the next dip can raise a fresh one.
  Future<void> raiseOrClearLowBalanceAlert({required String companyId, required String companyName, required double availableToSell}) async {
    if (!_isReal) return;
    final db = _db!;
    final ownerId = _auth!.currentOwnerId!;
    final existing = await (db.select(db.notificationsTable)
          ..where((t) =>
              t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) &
              t.relatedEntityId.equals(companyId) &
              t.type.equals(notificationTypeToRow(NotificationType.companyLowBalance))))
        .get();

    if (availableToSell > 0) {
      if (existing.isEmpty) return;
      for (final row in existing) {
        await _scheduler.cancel(row.osNotificationId);
        await (db.delete(db.notificationsTable)..where((t) => t.id.equals(row.id))).go();
        unawaited(_syncEngine?.recordAndPushDelete(entityTable: 'notifications', id: row.id, ownerId: ownerId) ?? Future.value());
      }
      await _loadFromDrift();
      return;
    }

    if (existing.isNotEmpty) return;
    await _insertAndSchedule(
      type: NotificationType.companyLowBalance,
      title: 'notifications.companyLowBalance.title'.tr(),
      body: 'notifications.companyLowBalance.body'.tr(namedArgs: {'company': companyName, 'amount': availableToSell.toStringAsFixed(2)}),
      relatedEntityId: companyId,
      fireAt: DateTime.now(),
    );
  }

  // ── Company owed (unpaid shipment) ──────────────────────────────────

  Future<void> notifyCompanyOwed({required String companyId, required String companyName, required double amountOwed}) async {
    if (!_isReal || amountOwed <= 0) return;
    await _insertAndSchedule(
      type: NotificationType.companyOwedAlert,
      title: 'notifications.companyOwedAlert.title'.tr(),
      body: 'notifications.companyOwedAlert.body'.tr(namedArgs: {'company': companyName, 'amount': amountOwed.toStringAsFixed(2)}),
      relatedEntityId: companyId,
      fireAt: DateTime.now(),
    );
  }

  // ── Admin: new support request (live, not scheduled) ────────────────

  /// Called by `SupportRequestRealtimeGate` the instant a seller submits
  /// a new Support & Feedback request, while an admin's app is open.
  /// Unlike every other notification here, nothing schedules this ahead
  /// of time — it's a plain immediate insert (`fireAt: DateTime.now()`),
  /// same underlying path (`_insertAndSchedule` already shows instantly
  /// via `NotificationScheduler.showNow` when `fireAt` isn't in the
  /// future) that a same-instant company-balance alert already uses.
  Future<void> notifyNewSupportRequest({required String requestTypeLabel}) async {
    if (!_isReal) return;
    await _insertAndSchedule(
      type: NotificationType.newSupportRequest,
      title: 'notifications.newSupportRequest.title'.tr(),
      body: 'notifications.newSupportRequest.body'.tr(namedArgs: {'type': requestTypeLabel}),
      relatedEntityId: null,
      fireAt: DateTime.now(),
    );
  }

  /// Call whenever a company stops being active (archived, soft-deleted,
  /// or permanently deleted) — `raiseOrClearLowBalanceAlert` only ever
  /// runs over the *active* company list, so without this a low-balance
  /// alert already raised before the company left that list would sit
  /// in the inbox forever pointing at a company that's gone.
  Future<void> cancelCompanyAlerts(String companyId) async {
    if (!_isReal) return;
    await _cancelWhere((row) =>
        row.relatedEntityId == companyId &&
        (row.type == notificationTypeToRow(NotificationType.companyLowBalance) ||
            row.type == notificationTypeToRow(NotificationType.companyOwedAlert)));
  }
}
===== D:\Android\sim_sales_manager\lib\core\notifications\notifications_state.dart =====
import 'package:equatable/equatable.dart';

import 'app_notification.dart';

class NotificationsState extends Equatable {
  final List<AppNotification> notifications;

  const NotificationsState({this.notifications = const []});

  /// The inbox list — deliberately excludes anything still pending
  /// (`fireAt` in the future): a notification whose title/body says
  /// "expires today" or "3 days left" is only true at the moment it
  /// actually fires, so showing it ahead of time (labelled "scheduled
  /// for `future date`") reads as contradictory, not as a preview. This
  /// mirrors how a real OS notification tray works — nothing appears
  /// until it actually happens. `NotificationsCubit.pendingDebtReminder`
  /// still reads the raw `notifications` list directly, so a still-future
  /// debt reminder stays fully trackable from the customer detail page;
  /// it's only hidden from this general inbox.
  List<AppNotification> get sortedByNewest =>
      notifications.where((n) => !n.isPending).toList()..sort((a, b) => b.fireAt.compareTo(a.fireAt));

  /// Only counts ones that have actually fired — a still-pending future
  /// reminder hasn't "happened" yet, so it shouldn't inflate the badge.
  int get unreadCount => notifications.where((n) => !n.read && !n.isPending).length;

  NotificationsState copyWith({List<AppNotification>? notifications}) {
    return NotificationsState(notifications: notifications ?? this.notifications);
  }

  @override
  List<Object?> get props => [notifications];
}

===== D:\Android\sim_sales_manager\lib\core\settings\settings_cubit.dart =====
import 'dart:async';

import 'package:drift/drift.dart';
import 'package:flutter_bloc/flutter_bloc.dart';

import '../activity_log/activity_action_type.dart';
import '../activity_log/activity_logger.dart';
import '../auth/auth_service.dart';
import '../config/env.dart';
import '../database/app_database.dart';
import '../di/service_locator.dart';
import '../sync/connectivity_service.dart';
import '../sync/sync_engine.dart';
import 'app_settings.dart';

/// Real mode persists `currencySymbol` to Drift's `settings` table (one
/// row per owner, synced to Supabase like every other tenant table) —
/// same conditional real/mock pattern as the rest of Phase 3. Falls back
/// to a runtime-only default offline/in demo mode.
class SettingsCubit extends Cubit<AppSettings> {
  final AppDatabase? _db;
  final AuthService? _auth;
  final SyncEngine? _syncEngine;
  final ConnectivityService? _connectivity;

  SettingsCubit({AppDatabase? db, AuthService? auth, SyncEngine? syncEngine, ConnectivityService? connectivity})
    : _db = _resolveDb(db),
      _auth = _resolveAuth(auth),
      _syncEngine = syncEngine ?? (Env.isConfigured ? getIt<SyncEngine>() : null),
      _connectivity = connectivity ?? getIt<ConnectivityService>(),
      super(const AppSettings()) {
    if (_db != null && _auth?.currentOwnerId != null) {
      _syncThenLoad();
    }
  }

  static AppDatabase? _resolveDb(AppDatabase? db) => db ?? (Env.isConfigured ? getIt<AppDatabase>() : null);
  static AuthService? _resolveAuth(AuthService? auth) => auth ?? (Env.isConfigured ? getIt<AuthService>() : null);

  bool get _isReal => _db != null && _auth?.currentOwnerId != null;

  Future<void> _syncThenLoad() async {
    final engine = _syncEngine;
    final ownerId = _auth?.currentOwnerId;
    // Checking connectivity first (a cheap, instant OS-level flag) skips
    // even attempting the network call while offline — without it, this
    // waited out a multi-second connect/DNS timeout before falling back
    // every single time.
    if (engine != null && ownerId != null && await (_connectivity?.isOnline ?? Future.value(true))) {
      try {
        await engine.pushPendingSettings(ownerId);
        await engine.pullSettings(ownerId);
      } catch (_) {
        // Offline or transient failure — proceed with local data.
      }
    }
    await _loadFromDrift();
  }

  Future<void> _loadFromDrift() async {
    final db = _db!;
    final ownerId = _auth!.currentOwnerId!;
    final row = await (db.select(db.settingsTable)..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId))).getSingleOrNull();
    if (row != null) emit(state.copyWith(currencySymbol: row.currencySymbol));
  }

  void updateCurrencySymbol(String symbol) {
    final trimmed = symbol.trim();
    if (trimmed.isEmpty) return;
    emit(state.copyWith(currencySymbol: trimmed));
    if (_isReal) unawaited(_persist());
    getIt<ActivityLogger>().log(ActivityActionType.edit, 'Changed the app currency symbol to $trimmed.');
  }

  Future<void> _persist() async {
    final db = _db!;
    final ownerId = _auth!.currentOwnerId!;
    try {
      await db.into(db.settingsTable).insert(
            SettingsTableCompanion.insert(
              ownerId: ownerId,
              currencySymbol: Value(state.currencySymbol),
              synced: const Value(false),
              updatedAt: DateTime.now(),
            ),
            mode: InsertMode.insertOrReplace,
          );
      if (_syncEngine != null) {
        await _syncEngine.pushPendingSettings(ownerId);
      }
    } catch (_) {
      // Offline or transient failure — row stays local, next sync retries.
    }
  }
}

===== D:\Android\sim_sales_manager\lib\core\sync\connectivity_service.dart =====
import 'dart:io';

import 'package:connectivity_plus/connectivity_plus.dart';

/// Thin wrapper around `connectivity_plus` exposing a plain online/offline
/// stream — `SyncBloc` reacts to this rather than every feature touching
/// `connectivity_plus` directly.
///
/// `connectivity_plus` alone only reports whether a network *adapter*
/// (WiFi, mobile data, ...) is connected — a WiFi router with no working
/// internet uplink still reports "connected" there, which used to make
/// every real/mock cubit's connectivity pre-check pass, then hang on the
/// platform's much longer default socket-connect timeout once it tried
/// an actual Supabase call. A quick DNS lookup confirms real reachability
/// without risking that long stall. The timeout is 3s, not 1s — mobile
/// data (weak signal especially) routinely takes longer than 1s to
/// resolve a DNS query even when it's genuinely online, and a 1s cap was
/// misreporting those as offline, silently skipping real sync
/// opportunities (see PROGRESS.md's "mobile data sync" entry). 3s is
/// still fast enough to never read as a stall to the user.
class ConnectivityService {
  final Connectivity _connectivity;

  ConnectivityService({Connectivity? connectivity}) : _connectivity = connectivity ?? Connectivity();

  static const _probeTimeout = Duration(seconds: 3);

  Stream<bool> get onConnectivityChanged => _connectivity.onConnectivityChanged.asyncMap(_hasRealInternet);

  Future<bool> get isOnline async => _hasRealInternet(await _connectivity.checkConnectivity());

  bool _hasAdapter(List<ConnectivityResult> results) => results.any((r) => r != ConnectivityResult.none);

  Future<bool> _hasRealInternet(List<ConnectivityResult> results) async {
    if (!_hasAdapter(results)) return false;
    try {
      final addresses = await InternetAddress.lookup('supabase.co').timeout(_probeTimeout);
      return addresses.isNotEmpty && addresses.first.rawAddress.isNotEmpty;
    } catch (_) {
      return false;
    }
  }
}
===== D:\Android\sim_sales_manager\lib\core\sync\sync_engine.dart =====
import 'package:drift/drift.dart';
import 'package:supabase_flutter/supabase_flutter.dart';

import '../database/app_database.dart';

/// Push/pull between the local Drift database and Supabase.
///
/// **Catalog** (`company_catalog`/`package_catalog`): read-only pull —
/// sellers never write it, admins write it directly via Supabase and it
/// flows down here like any other pull.
///
/// **Tenant tables** (`companies`/`packages`/`customers`/`lines`/
/// `shipments`/`settings` so far — more get added
/// here one at a time as each feature migrates off mock data, see
/// PROGRESS.md "Next"): push rows with `synced = false` via `upsert`,
/// then mark them synced locally; pull replaces local rows with
/// whatever the server has for this owner. **Simplification, on
/// purpose** (per CLAUDE.md: don't over-engineer conflict resolution) —
/// push always runs before pull in [syncAll], so by the time pull runs
/// this device's own edits are already reflected server-side; there's no
/// separate `updated_at` comparison on pull, and no tombstone tracking
/// for permanent deletes made on *another* device (a row deleted
/// elsewhere while this device was offline will reappear on next pull —
/// acceptable for a single-seller-across-devices scenario, revisit if
/// that turns out to matter in practice).
class SyncEngine {
  final SupabaseClient _client;
  final AppDatabase _db;

  SyncEngine(this._client, this._db);

  /// How many local rows for [ownerId] still haven't reached Supabase —
  /// every `synced = false` row across every synced table, plus any
  /// permanent-delete tombstone still waiting to confirm remotely (see
  /// `pending_deletes_table.dart`). Drives the prominent "not backed up
  /// yet" indicator (`SyncBloc`/`SyncStatusIndicator`) — the whole point
  /// is a seller can tell, at a glance, whether it's safe to do
  /// something drastic (clear app storage, uninstall, switch devices)
  /// without silently losing work that never made it to the server.
  Future<int> countPendingChanges(String ownerId) async {
    var count = 0;
    count += (await (_db.select(_db.companiesTable)..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false))).get()).length;
    count += (await (_db.select(_db.packagesTable)..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false))).get()).length;
    count += (await (_db.select(_db.customersTable)..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false))).get()).length;
    count += (await (_db.select(_db.linesTable)..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false))).get()).length;
    count += (await (_db.select(_db.shipmentsTable)..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false))).get()).length;
    count += (await (_db.select(_db.settingsTable)..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false))).get()).length;
    count += (await (_db.select(_db.activityLogTable)..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false))).get()).length;
    count += (await (_db.select(_db.supportRequestsTable)..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false))).get()).length;
    count += (await (_db.select(_db.numberUpdateLogTable)..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false))).get()).length;
    count +=
        (await (_db.select(_db.numberUpdateProviderCacheTable)..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false))).get()).length;
    count += (await (_db.select(_db.notificationsTable)..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false))).get()).length;
    count += (await (_db.select(_db.pendingDeletesTable)..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId))).get()).length;
    return count;
  }

  /// Runs a full sync for [ownerId]: catalog pull, then push/pull for
  /// every tenant table currently migrated. Companies before packages
  /// (packages reference companies by id); customers before lines (lines
  /// reference both a customer and a company).
  ///
  /// Each step is isolated (`_step`, below) — before this, a single
  /// table throwing (a transient error, or a genuinely bad row) aborted
  /// the *entire* remaining chain, since nothing here had its own
  /// try/catch: an early failure silently skipped every step after it,
  /// including whichever tables' pending rows might otherwise have
  /// synced cleanly. That's exactly the shape of bug that could leave
  /// the "not backed up yet" count stuck long after the actual problem
  /// (if any) was long gone — a real "sync now" press should never come
  /// back having accomplished nothing just because one unrelated table
  /// had a bad moment.
  Future<void> syncAll(String ownerId) async {
    await _step(pullCatalog);
    await _step(() => pushPendingCompanies(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pushPendingPackages(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pushPendingCustomers(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pushPendingLines(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pushPendingAdditionalServices(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pushPendingShipments(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pushPendingSettings(ownerId));
    // Flush any permanent deletes still waiting on their remote
    // counterpart *before* pulling — otherwise a delete that hadn't
    // reached the server yet gets resurrected by the pull below re-
    // fetching a row that (from this device's perspective) is already
    // gone. See pending_deletes_table.dart.
    await _step(() => pushPendingDeletes(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pullCompanies(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pullPackages(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pullCustomers(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pullLines(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pullAdditionalServices(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pushPendingActivityLog(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pushPendingSupportRequests(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pushPendingNumberUpdateLog(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pullShipments(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pullSettings(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pullActivityLog(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pullSupportRequests(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pullNumberUpdateLog(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pushPendingNotifications(ownerId));
    await _step(() => pullNotifications(ownerId));
  }

  Future<void> _step(Future<void> Function() step) async {
    try {
      await step();
    } catch (_) {
      // Offline or a transient/one-off failure on this specific table —
      // every other, independent step still gets its own chance to run.
      // Whatever failed here just stays `synced = false` for the next
      // sync attempt to pick up, same as always.
    }
  }

  /// Tombstones a permanent local delete of [id] from [entityTable] and
  /// immediately attempts the matching remote delete — most of the time
  /// the tombstone is gone again by the time this returns. It only
  /// lingers when the attempt fails (offline/transient), in which case
  /// [pushPendingDeletes] retries it on the next sync; either way, a
  /// pull can never resurrect the row in between, since every pull here
  /// filters out ids still tombstoned. Callers: a permanent delete in
  /// `CompaniesCubit`/`CustomersCubit` (companies, customers, lines).
  Future<void> recordAndPushDelete({required String entityTable, required String id, required String ownerId}) async {
    await _db.into(_db.pendingDeletesTable).insertOnConflictUpdate(
          PendingDeletesTableCompanion.insert(entityTable: entityTable, entityId: id, ownerId: ownerId),
        );
    await _flushOneDelete(entityTable, id);
  }

  /// Retries every delete still waiting on server confirmation — called
  /// as part of [syncAll], before any pull.
  Future<void> pushPendingDeletes(String ownerId) async {
    final tombstones = await (_db.select(_db.pendingDeletesTable)..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId))).get();
    for (final t in tombstones) {
      await _flushOneDelete(t.entityTable, t.entityId);
    }
  }

  Future<void> _flushOneDelete(String entityTable, String id) async {
    try {
      await _client.from(entityTable).delete().eq('id', id);
      await (_db.delete(_db.pendingDeletesTable)
            ..where((t) => t.entityTable.equals(entityTable) & t.entityId.equals(id)))
          .go();
    } catch (_) {
      // Still offline or a transient failure — tombstone stays, retried
      // by the next `pushPendingDeletes` call.
    }
  }

  Future<Set<String>> _tombstonedIds(String ownerId, String entityTable) async {
    final rows = await (_db.select(_db.pendingDeletesTable)
          ..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.entityTable.equals(entityTable)))
        .get();
    return rows.map((r) => r.entityId).toSet();
  }

  /// Pulls the full company/package catalog. Small, shared, admin-edited
  /// rarely — a full replace on every sync is simpler and cheap enough to
  /// not need incremental `updated_at` filtering yet.
  Future<void> pullCatalog() async {
    final companies = await _client.from('company_catalog').select();
    await _db.batch((batch) {
      batch.insertAllOnConflictUpdate(
        _db.companyCatalogTable,
        companies.map(_catalogCompanyFromRow).toList(),
      );
    });

    final packages = await _client.from('package_catalog').select();
    await _db.batch((batch) {
      batch.insertAllOnConflictUpdate(
        _db.packageCatalogTable,
        packages.map(_catalogPackageFromRow).toList(),
      );
    });
  }

  // ── companies ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Future<void> pushPendingCompanies(String ownerId) async {
    final pending = await (_db.select(_db.companiesTable)
          ..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false)))
        .get();
    if (pending.isEmpty) return;

    await _client.from('companies').upsert(pending.map(_companyToRow).toList());

    for (final row in pending) {
      await (_db.update(_db.companiesTable)..where((t) => t.id.equals(row.id)))
          .write(const CompaniesTableCompanion(synced: Value(true)));
    }
  }

  Future<void> pullCompanies(String ownerId) async {
    final rows = await _client.from('companies').select().eq('owner_id', ownerId);
    if (rows.isEmpty) return;
    final tombstoned = await _tombstonedIds(ownerId, 'companies');
    final fresh = tombstoned.isEmpty ? rows : rows.where((r) => !tombstoned.contains(r['id'])).toList();
    if (fresh.isEmpty) return;
    await _db.batch((batch) {
      batch.insertAllOnConflictUpdate(_db.companiesTable, fresh.map(_companyFromRow).toList());
    });
  }

  Map<String, dynamic> _companyToRow(CompaniesTableData c) => {
        'id': c.id,
        'owner_id': c.ownerId,
        'name': c.name,
        'logo_url': c.logoUrl,
        'notes': c.notes,
        'status': c.status,
        'pinned': c.pinned,
        'catalog_company_id': c.catalogCompanyId,
        'number_update_provider': c.numberUpdateProvider,
        'number_update_url': c.numberUpdateUrl,
        'allow_negative_balance': c.allowNegativeBalance,
        'updated_at': c.updatedAt.toUtc().toIso8601String(),
      };

  CompaniesTableCompanion _companyFromRow(Map<String, dynamic> row) {
    return CompaniesTableCompanion.insert(
      id: row['id'] as String,
      ownerId: row['owner_id'] as String,
      name: row['name'] as String,
      logoUrl: Value(row['logo_url'] as String?),
      notes: Value(row['notes'] as String?),
      status: Value(row['status'] as String? ?? 'active'),
      pinned: Value(row['pinned'] as bool? ?? false),
      catalogCompanyId: Value(row['catalog_company_id'] as String?),
      numberUpdateProvider: Value(row['number_update_provider'] as String?),
      numberUpdateUrl: Value(row['number_update_url'] as String?),
      allowNegativeBalance: Value(row['allow_negative_balance'] as bool? ?? false),
      updatedAt: DateTime.parse(row['updated_at'] as String).toLocal(),
    );
  }

  // ── packages ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Future<void> pushPendingPackages(String ownerId) async {
    final pending = await (_db.select(_db.packagesTable)
          ..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false)))
        .get();
    if (pending.isEmpty) return;

    await _client.from('packages').upsert(pending.map(_packageToRow).toList());

    for (final row in pending) {
      await (_db.update(_db.packagesTable)..where((t) => t.id.equals(row.id)))
          .write(const PackagesTableCompanion(synced: Value(true)));
    }
  }

  Future<void> pullPackages(String ownerId) async {
    final rows = await _client.from('packages').select().eq('owner_id', ownerId);
    if (rows.isEmpty) return;
    await _db.batch((batch) {
      batch.insertAllOnConflictUpdate(_db.packagesTable, rows.map(_packageFromRow).toList());
    });
  }

  Map<String, dynamic> _packageToRow(PackagesTableData p) => {
        'id': p.id,
        'owner_id': p.ownerId,
        'company_id': p.companyId,
        'name': p.name,
        'sim_type': p.simType,
        'wholesale_cost': p.wholesaleCost,
        'retail_price': p.retailPrice,
        'updated_at': p.updatedAt.toUtc().toIso8601String(),
      };

  PackagesTableCompanion _packageFromRow(Map<String, dynamic> row) {
    return PackagesTableCompanion.insert(
      id: row['id'] as String,
      ownerId: row['owner_id'] as String,
      companyId: row['company_id'] as String,
      name: row['name'] as String,
      simType: Value(row['sim_type'] as String?),
      wholesaleCost: Value((row['wholesale_cost'] as num).toDouble()),
      retailPrice: Value((row['retail_price'] as num).toDouble()),
      updatedAt: DateTime.parse(row['updated_at'] as String).toLocal(),
    );
  }

  // ── customers ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Future<void> pushPendingCustomers(String ownerId) async {
    final pending = await (_db.select(_db.customersTable)
          ..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false)))
        .get();
    if (pending.isEmpty) return;

    await _client.from('customers').upsert(pending.map(_customerToRow).toList());

    for (final row in pending) {
      await (_db.update(_db.customersTable)..where((t) => t.id.equals(row.id)))
          .write(const CustomersTableCompanion(synced: Value(true)));
    }
  }

  Future<void> pullCustomers(String ownerId) async {
    final rows = await _client.from('customers').select().eq('owner_id', ownerId);
    if (rows.isEmpty) return;
    final tombstoned = await _tombstonedIds(ownerId, 'customers');
    final fresh = tombstoned.isEmpty ? rows : rows.where((r) => !tombstoned.contains(r['id'])).toList();
    if (fresh.isEmpty) return;
    await _db.batch((batch) {
      batch.insertAllOnConflictUpdate(_db.customersTable, fresh.map(_customerFromRow).toList());
    });
  }

  Map<String, dynamic> _customerToRow(CustomersTableData c) => {
        'id': c.id,
        'owner_id': c.ownerId,
        'name': c.name,
        'contact_number': c.contactNumber,
        'status': c.status,
        // Local storage is a comma-joined string (see tables/customers_table.dart);
        // Postgres' column is a real `text[]`, so this is the one spot that
        // has to convert between the two representations.
        'tags': c.tags.isEmpty ? const <String>[] : c.tags.split(','),
        'pinned': c.pinned,
        'notes': c.notes,
        'deleted': c.deleted,
        'updated_at': c.updatedAt.toUtc().toIso8601String(),
      };

  CustomersTableCompanion _customerFromRow(Map<String, dynamic> row) {
    final tags = (row['tags'] as List?)?.cast<String>() ?? const <String>[];
    return CustomersTableCompanion.insert(
      id: row['id'] as String,
      ownerId: row['owner_id'] as String,
      name: row['name'] as String,
      contactNumber: row['contact_number'] as String,
      status: Value(row['status'] as String? ?? 'normal'),
      tags: Value(tags.join(',')),
      pinned: Value(row['pinned'] as bool? ?? false),
      notes: Value(row['notes'] as String?),
      deleted: Value(row['deleted'] as bool? ?? false),
      updatedAt: DateTime.parse(row['updated_at'] as String).toLocal(),
    );
  }

  // ── lines ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Future<void> pushPendingLines(String ownerId) async {
    final pending = await (_db.select(_db.linesTable)
          ..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false)))
        .get();
    if (pending.isEmpty) return;

    await _client.from('lines').upsert(pending.map(_lineToRow).toList());

    for (final row in pending) {
      await (_db.update(_db.linesTable)..where((t) => t.id.equals(row.id)))
          .write(const LinesTableCompanion(synced: Value(true)));
    }
  }

  Future<void> pullLines(String ownerId) async {
    final rows = await _client.from('lines').select().eq('owner_id', ownerId);
    if (rows.isEmpty) return;
    final tombstoned = await _tombstonedIds(ownerId, 'lines');
    final fresh = tombstoned.isEmpty ? rows : rows.where((r) => !tombstoned.contains(r['id'])).toList();
    if (fresh.isEmpty) return;
    await _db.batch((batch) {
      batch.insertAllOnConflictUpdate(_db.linesTable, fresh.map(_lineFromRow).toList());
    });
  }

  Map<String, dynamic> _lineToRow(LinesTableData l) => {
        'id': l.id,
        'owner_id': l.ownerId,
        'customer_id': l.customerId,
        'company_id': l.companyId,
        'company_name': l.companyName,
        'company_initials': l.companyInitials,
        'package_id': l.packageId,
        'package_name': l.packageName,
        'package_sim_type': l.packageSimType,
        'sim_number': l.simNumber,
        'purchase_date': l.purchaseDate.toUtc().toIso8601String(),
        'expiry_date': l.expiryDate.toUtc().toIso8601String(),
        'wholesale_cost': l.wholesaleCost,
        'retail_price': l.retailPrice,
        'profit': l.profit,
        'payment_status': l.paymentStatus,
        'amount_paid': l.amountPaid,
        'amount_remaining': l.amountRemaining,
        'updated_at': l.updatedAt.toUtc().toIso8601String(),
      };

  LinesTableCompanion _lineFromRow(Map<String, dynamic> row) {
    return LinesTableCompanion.insert(
      id: row['id'] as String,
      ownerId: row['owner_id'] as String,
      customerId: row['customer_id'] as String,
      companyId: Value(row['company_id'] as String?),
      companyName: Value(row['company_name'] as String?),
      companyInitials: Value(row['company_initials'] as String?),
      packageId: Value(row['package_id'] as String?),
      packageName: Value(row['package_name'] as String?),
      packageSimType: Value(row['package_sim_type'] as String?),
      simNumber: row['sim_number'] as String,
      purchaseDate: DateTime.parse(row['purchase_date'] as String).toLocal(),
      expiryDate: DateTime.parse(row['expiry_date'] as String).toLocal(),
      wholesaleCost: Value((row['wholesale_cost'] as num).toDouble()),
      retailPrice: Value((row['retail_price'] as num).toDouble()),
      profit: Value((row['profit'] as num).toDouble()),
      paymentStatus: Value(row['payment_status'] as String? ?? 'unpaid'),
      amountPaid: Value((row['amount_paid'] as num).toDouble()),
      amountRemaining: Value((row['amount_remaining'] as num).toDouble()),
      updatedAt: DateTime.parse(row['updated_at'] as String).toLocal(),
    );
  }

  // ── additional services ──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Future<void> pushPendingAdditionalServices(String ownerId) async {
    final pending = await (_db.select(_db.additionalServicesTable)
          ..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false)))
        .get();
    if (pending.isEmpty) return;

    await _client.from('additional_services').upsert(pending.map(_additionalServiceToRow).toList());

    for (final row in pending) {
      await (_db.update(_db.additionalServicesTable)..where((t) => t.id.equals(row.id)))
          .write(const AdditionalServicesTableCompanion(synced: Value(true)));
    }
  }

  Future<void> pullAdditionalServices(String ownerId) async {
    final rows = await _client.from('additional_services').select().eq('owner_id', ownerId);
    if (rows.isEmpty) return;
    final tombstoned = await _tombstonedIds(ownerId, 'additional_services');
    final fresh = tombstoned.isEmpty ? rows : rows.where((r) => !tombstoned.contains(r['id'])).toList();
    if (fresh.isEmpty) return;
    await _db.batch((batch) {
      batch.insertAllOnConflictUpdate(_db.additionalServicesTable, fresh.map(_additionalServiceFromRow).toList());
    });
  }

  Map<String, dynamic> _additionalServiceToRow(AdditionalServicesTableData s) => {
        'id': s.id,
        'owner_id': s.ownerId,
        'line_id': s.lineId,
        'customer_id': s.customerId,
        'company_id': s.companyId,
        'amount_deducted': s.amountDeducted,
        'amount_charged': s.amountCharged,
        'gb_added': s.gbAdded,
        'collected': s.collected,
        'created_at': s.createdAt.toUtc().toIso8601String(),
        'updated_at': s.updatedAt.toUtc().toIso8601String(),
      };

  AdditionalServicesTableCompanion _additionalServiceFromRow(Map<String, dynamic> row) {
    return AdditionalServicesTableCompanion.insert(
      id: row['id'] as String,
      ownerId: row['owner_id'] as String,
      lineId: row['line_id'] as String,
      customerId: row['customer_id'] as String,
      companyId: Value(row['company_id'] as String?),
      amountDeducted: (row['amount_deducted'] as num).toDouble(),
      amountCharged: Value((row['amount_charged'] as num).toDouble()),
      gbAdded: Value((row['gb_added'] as num).toDouble()),
      collected: Value(row['collected'] as bool? ?? false),
      createdAt: DateTime.parse(row['created_at'] as String).toLocal(),
      updatedAt: DateTime.parse(row['updated_at'] as String).toLocal(),
    );
  }

  // ── shipments ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Future<void> pushPendingShipments(String ownerId) async {
    final pending = await (_db.select(_db.shipmentsTable)
          ..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false)))
        .get();
    if (pending.isEmpty) return;

    await _client.from('shipments').upsert(pending.map(_shipmentToRow).toList());

    for (final row in pending) {
      await (_db.update(_db.shipmentsTable)..where((t) => t.id.equals(row.id)))
          .write(const ShipmentsTableCompanion(synced: Value(true)));
    }
  }

  Future<void> pullShipments(String ownerId) async {
    final rows = await _client.from('shipments').select().eq('owner_id', ownerId);
    if (rows.isEmpty) return;
    await _db.batch((batch) {
      batch.insertAllOnConflictUpdate(_db.shipmentsTable, rows.map(_shipmentFromRow).toList());
    });
  }

  Map<String, dynamic> _shipmentToRow(ShipmentsTableData s) => {
        'id': s.id,
        'owner_id': s.ownerId,
        'company_id': s.companyId,
        'type': s.type,
        'total_amount': s.totalAmount,
        'amount_paid': s.amountPaid,
        'amount_remaining': s.amountRemaining,
        'payment_method': s.paymentMethod,
        'reference_number': s.referenceNumber,
        'shipment_date': s.shipmentDate.toUtc().toIso8601String(),
        'notes': s.notes,
        'updated_at': s.updatedAt.toUtc().toIso8601String(),
      };

  ShipmentsTableCompanion _shipmentFromRow(Map<String, dynamic> row) {
    return ShipmentsTableCompanion.insert(
      id: row['id'] as String,
      ownerId: row['owner_id'] as String,
      companyId: row['company_id'] as String,
      type: row['type'] as String,
      totalAmount: Value((row['total_amount'] as num).toDouble()),
      amountPaid: Value((row['amount_paid'] as num).toDouble()),
      amountRemaining: Value((row['amount_remaining'] as num).toDouble()),
      paymentMethod: Value(row['payment_method'] as String?),
      referenceNumber: Value(row['reference_number'] as String?),
      shipmentDate: DateTime.parse(row['shipment_date'] as String).toLocal(),
      notes: Value(row['notes'] as String?),
      updatedAt: DateTime.parse(row['updated_at'] as String).toLocal(),
    );
  }

  // ── settings ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  // One row per owner (the primary key *is* `owner_id`), so this is
  // simpler than the others: no per-row loop, just a single upsert/read.

  Future<void> pushPendingSettings(String ownerId) async {
    final row = await (_db.select(_db.settingsTable)..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false)))
        .getSingleOrNull();
    if (row == null) return;

    await _client.from('settings').upsert(_settingsToRow(row));
    await (_db.update(_db.settingsTable)..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId)))
        .write(const SettingsTableCompanion(synced: Value(true)));
  }

  Future<void> pullSettings(String ownerId) async {
    final rows = await _client.from('settings').select().eq('owner_id', ownerId);
    if (rows.isEmpty) return;
    await _db.batch((batch) {
      batch.insertAllOnConflictUpdate(_db.settingsTable, rows.map(_settingsFromRow).toList());
    });
  }

  Map<String, dynamic> _settingsToRow(SettingsTableData s) => {
        'owner_id': s.ownerId,
        'currency_symbol': s.currencySymbol,
        'theme_preference': s.themePreference,
        'updated_at': s.updatedAt.toUtc().toIso8601String(),
      };

  SettingsTableCompanion _settingsFromRow(Map<String, dynamic> row) {
    return SettingsTableCompanion.insert(
      ownerId: row['owner_id'] as String,
      currencySymbol: Value(row['currency_symbol'] as String? ?? '₪'),
      themePreference: Value(row['theme_preference'] as String? ?? 'system'),
      updatedAt: DateTime.parse(row['updated_at'] as String).toLocal(),
    );
  }

  // ── support requests ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
  // Append-only, same shape as activity log — a submitted request is
  // never edited locally afterward. `attachment_url`/`voice_note_url`
  // hold a `support-attachments` Storage object path (not a public URL —
  // the bucket is private), uploaded by `SupportFeedbackCubit` before
  // this table row is even written; this push/pull just carries whatever
  // path ends up in the local row, same as any other column.

  Future<void> pushPendingSupportRequests(String ownerId) async {
    final pending = await (_db.select(_db.supportRequestsTable)
          ..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false)))
        .get();
    if (pending.isEmpty) return;

    await _client.from('support_requests').upsert(pending.map(_supportRequestToRow).toList());

    for (final row in pending) {
      await (_db.update(_db.supportRequestsTable)..where((t) => t.id.equals(row.id)))
          .write(const SupportRequestsTableCompanion(synced: Value(true)));
    }
  }

  Future<void> pullSupportRequests(String ownerId) async {
    final rows = await _client.from('support_requests').select().eq('owner_id', ownerId);
    if (rows.isEmpty) return;
    await _db.batch((batch) {
      batch.insertAllOnConflictUpdate(_db.supportRequestsTable, rows.map(_supportRequestFromRow).toList());
    });
  }

  Map<String, dynamic> _supportRequestToRow(SupportRequestsTableData s) => {
        'id': s.id,
        'owner_id': s.ownerId,
        'type': s.type,
        'body': s.body,
        'attachment_url': s.attachmentUrl,
        'voice_note_url': s.voiceNoteUrl,
        'created_at': s.createdAt.toUtc().toIso8601String(),
        'updated_at': s.updatedAt.toUtc().toIso8601String(),
      };

  SupportRequestsTableCompanion _supportRequestFromRow(Map<String, dynamic> row) {
    return SupportRequestsTableCompanion.insert(
      id: row['id'] as String,
      ownerId: row['owner_id'] as String,
      type: row['type'] as String,
      body: row['body'] as String,
      attachmentUrl: Value(row['attachment_url'] as String?),
      voiceNoteUrl: Value(row['voice_note_url'] as String?),
      createdAt: DateTime.parse(row['created_at'] as String).toLocal(),
      updatedAt: DateTime.parse(row['updated_at'] as String).toLocal(),
    );
  }

  // ── activity log ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  // Append-only audit trail (see `ActivityLogger`) — no local update

      batch.insertAllOnConflictUpdate(_db.activityLogTable, rows.map(_activityLogFromRow).toList());

  }

  // ── notifications ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  // Backs up the local notifications inbox/schedule so a still-future
  // reminder survives clearing the app's local storage — see
  // supabase/migrations/0015_notifications.sql. Same push/pull/tombstone
  // shape as companies/customers/lines; deletes (a cancelled reminder —
  // debt settled, balance recovered, line removed) go through
  // `recordAndPushDelete`/`pushPendingDeletes` like those tables too.

  Future<void> pushPendingNotifications(String ownerId) async {
    final pending = await (_db.select(_db.notificationsTable)
          ..where((t) => t.ownerId.equals(ownerId) & t.synced.equals(false)))
        .get();
    if (pending.isEmpty) return;

    await _client.from('notifications').upsert(pending.map(_notificationToRow).toList());

    for (final row in pending) {
      await (_db.update(_db.notificationsTable)..where((t) => t.id.equals(row.id)))
          .write(const NotificationsTableCompanion(synced: Value(true)));
    }
  }

  Future<void> pullNotifications(String ownerId) async {
    final rows = await _client.from('notifications').select().eq('owner_id', ownerId);
    if (rows.isEmpty) return;
    final tombstoned = await _tombstonedIds(ownerId, 'notifications');
    final fresh = tombstoned.isEmpty ? rows : rows.where((r) => !tombstoned.contains(r['id'])).toList();
    if (fresh.isEmpty) return;
    await _db.batch((batch) {
      batch.insertAllOnConflictUpdate(_db.notificationsTable, fresh.map(_notificationFromRow).toList());
    });
  }

  Map<String, dynamic> _notificationToRow(NotificationsTableData n) => {
        'id': n.id,
        'owner_id': n.ownerId,
        'os_notification_id': n.osNotificationId,
        'type': n.type,
        'title': n.title,
        'body': n.body,
        'related_entity_id': n.relatedEntityId,
        'fire_at': n.fireAt.toUtc().toIso8601String(),
        'read': n.read,
        'updated_at': n.updatedAt.toUtc().toIso8601String(),
      };

  NotificationsTableCompanion _notificationFromRow(Map<String, dynamic> row) {
    return NotificationsTableCompanion.insert(
      id: row['id'] as String,
      ownerId: row['owner_id'] as String,
      osNotificationId: (row['os_notification_id'] as num).toInt(),
      type: row['type'] as String,
      title: row['title'] as String,
      body: row['body'] as String,
      relatedEntityId: Value(row['related_entity_id'] as String?),
      fireAt: DateTime.parse(row['fire_at'] as String).toLocal(),
      read: Value(row['read'] as bool? ?? false),
      updatedAt: DateTime.parse(row['updated_at'] as String).toLocal(),
    );
  }

  // ── catalog row mapping ──────────────────────────────────────────────

  CompanyCatalogTableCompanion _catalogCompanyFromRow(Map<String, dynamic> row) {
    return CompanyCatalogTableCompanion.insert(
      id: row['id'] as String,
      name: row['name'] as String,
      logoUrl: Value(row['logo_url'] as String?),
      notes: Value(row['notes'] as String?),
      numberUpdateUrl: Value(row['number_update_url'] as String?),
      numberUpdateProvider: Value(row['number_update_provider'] as String?),
      status: Value(row['status'] as String? ?? 'active'),
      createdAt: DateTime.parse(row['created_at'] as String).toLocal(),
      updatedAt: DateTime.parse(row['updated_at'] as String).toLocal(),
    );
  }

  PackageCatalogTableCompanion _catalogPackageFromRow(Map<String, dynamic> row) {
    return PackageCatalogTableCompanion.insert(
      id: row['id'] as String,
      companyCatalogId: row['company_catalog_id'] as String,
      name: row['name'] as String,
      simType: Value(row['sim_type'] as String?),

      suggestedRetailPrice: Value((row['suggested_retail_price'] as num).toDouble()),

      createdAt: DateTime.parse(row['created_at'] as String).toLocal(),
      updatedAt: DateTime.parse(row['updated_at'] as String).toLocal(),
    );
  }
}
===== D:\Android\sim_sales_manager\lib\core\sync\bloc\sync_bloc.dart =====
import 'dart:async';

import 'package:flutter_bloc/flutter_bloc.dart';

import '../../auth/auth_service.dart';
import '../connectivity_service.dart';
import '../sync_engine.dart';
import 'sync_event.dart';
import 'sync_state.dart';

/// Event-driven by design (per CLAUDE.md, unlike the rest of the app's
/// Cubits) — it reacts to an external connectivity stream rather than
/// direct UI calls, which is exactly the case CLAUDE.md carves out for
/// full Bloc over Cubit.
class SyncBloc extends Bloc<SyncEvent, SyncState> {
  final ConnectivityService _connectivity;
  final SyncEngine? _engine;
  final AuthService? _auth;
  StreamSubscription<bool>? _connectivitySubscription;
  Timer? _periodicTimer;

  /// Independent of write-time pushes/connectivity-change events/app-open
  /// syncs — see `PeriodicSyncTick`'s doc comment for why this exists at
  /// all: it's the safety net that catches up if every other sync
  /// opportunity happened to land during a bad-signal moment.
  static const _periodicInterval = Duration(minutes: 3);

  /// [engine]/[auth] are nullable so the bloc still works (reporting
  /// `offline` forever) when Supabase isn't configured yet — see
  /// `Env.isConfigured` and `service_locator.dart`. Without a signed-in
  /// owner (before login, or offline demo mode) only the shared catalog
  /// syncs — tenant tables need a real `owner_id` to scope to.
  SyncBloc({required ConnectivityService connectivity, SyncEngine? engine, AuthService? auth})
    : _connectivity = connectivity,
      _engine = engine,
      _auth = auth,
      super(const SyncState.initial()) {
    on<SyncStarted>(_onStarted);
    on<ConnectivityChanged>(_onConnectivityChanged);
    on<SyncRequested>(_onSyncRequested);
    on<PeriodicSyncTick>(_onPeriodicTick);
  }

  Future<void> _onStarted(SyncStarted event, Emitter<SyncState> emit) async {
    final isOnline = await _connectivity.isOnline;
    add(ConnectivityChanged(isOnline));
    _connectivitySubscription?.cancel();
    _connectivitySubscription = _connectivity.onConnectivityChanged.listen((online) => add(ConnectivityChanged(online)));
    _periodicTimer?.cancel();
    _periodicTimer = Timer.periodic(_periodicInterval, (_) => add(const PeriodicSyncTick()));
  }

  Future<void> _onConnectivityChanged(ConnectivityChanged event, Emitter<SyncState> emit) async {
    if (!event.isOnline) {
      emit(state.copyWith(status: SyncStatus.offline));
      await _refreshPendingCount(emit);
      return;
    }
    await _runSync(emit);
  }

  Future<void> _onSyncRequested(SyncRequested event, Emitter<SyncState> emit) async {
    if (state.status == SyncStatus.offline) return;
    await _runSync(emit);
  }

  /// Silent — no `syncing` state transition, so this never flickers the
  /// indicator or interrupts anything; it just quietly tries to catch up
  /// and updates the pending count either way.
  Future<void> _onPeriodicTick(PeriodicSyncTick event, Emitter<SyncState> emit) async {
    final engine = _engine;
    final ownerId = _auth?.currentOwnerId;
    if (engine == null || ownerId == null) return;
    if (!await _connectivity.isOnline) {
      await _refreshPendingCount(emit);
      return;
    }
    try {
      await engine.syncAll(ownerId);
      emit(state.copyWith(status: SyncStatus.idle, lastSyncedAt: DateTime.now()));
    } catch (_) {
      // Still offline or a transient failure — quietly retried next tick.
    }
    await _refreshPendingCount(emit);
  }

  Future<void> _runSync(Emitter<SyncState> emit) async {
    final engine = _engine;
    if (engine == null) {
      emit(state.copyWith(status: SyncStatus.idle));
      return;
    }
    emit(state.copyWith(status: SyncStatus.syncing));
    try {
      final ownerId = _auth?.currentOwnerId;
      if (ownerId != null) {
        await engine.syncAll(ownerId);
      } else {
        await engine.pullCatalog();
      }
      emit(state.copyWith(status: SyncStatus.idle, lastSyncedAt: DateTime.now()));
    } catch (e) {
      emit(state.copyWith(status: SyncStatus.error, errorMessage: e.toString()));
    }
    await _refreshPendingCount(emit);
  }

  Future<void> _refreshPendingCount(Emitter<SyncState> emit) async {
    final engine = _engine;
    final ownerId = _auth?.currentOwnerId;
    if (engine == null || ownerId == null) return;
    try {
      final count = await engine.countPendingChanges(ownerId);
      emit(state.copyWith(pendingCount: count));
    } catch (_) {
      // Best-effort — the indicator just keeps whatever count it last had.
    }
  }

  @override
  Future<void> close() {
    _connectivitySubscription?.cancel();
    _periodicTimer?.cancel();
    return super.close();
  }
}
===== D:\Android\sim_sales_manager\lib\core\sync\bloc\sync_event.dart =====
import 'package:equatable/equatable.dart';

sealed class SyncEvent extends Equatable {
  const SyncEvent();

  @override
  List<Object?> get props => [];
}

/// Starts the connectivity listener — call once at app startup.
class SyncStarted extends SyncEvent {
  const SyncStarted();
}

/// Dispatched internally from the connectivity stream subscription.
class ConnectivityChanged extends SyncEvent {
  final bool isOnline;
  const ConnectivityChanged(this.isOnline);

  @override
  List<Object?> get props => [isOnline];
}

/// Manual pull-to-refresh style trigger, independent of connectivity
/// changes (e.g. a "sync now" button).
class SyncRequested extends SyncEvent {
  const SyncRequested();
}

/// Fired by `SyncBloc`'s own internal timer every few minutes while the
/// app is open — a safety net independent of write-time pushes,
/// connectivity-change events, and app-open syncs: if every one of
/// those happened to land during a bad-signal moment (mobile data is
/// more prone to this than WiFi), this is what eventually catches up
/// instead of local changes sitting unsynced for an entire session.
class PeriodicSyncTick extends SyncEvent {
  const PeriodicSyncTick();
}
===== D:\Android\sim_sales_manager\lib\core\sync\bloc\sync_state.dart =====
import 'package:equatable/equatable.dart';

enum SyncStatus { offline, idle, syncing, error }

/// Drives the app bar's sync-status indicator (CLAUDE.md: "a subtle
/// sync-status indicator — never a blocking spinner").
class SyncState extends Equatable {
  final SyncStatus status;
  final DateTime? lastSyncedAt;
  final String? errorMessage;

  /// How many local rows haven't reached Supabase yet — see
  /// `SyncEngine.countPendingChanges`. Recomputed after every sync
  /// attempt (success, failure, or offline) so it stays accurate even
  /// while nothing's actively syncing; drives the "not backed up yet"
  /// indicator, distinct from [status] (a sync can be `idle` — nothing
  /// running right now — while still carrying pending rows it just
  /// hasn't gotten to push yet).
  final int pendingCount;

  const SyncState({required this.status, this.lastSyncedAt, this.errorMessage, this.pendingCount = 0});

  const SyncState.initial() : this(status: SyncStatus.offline);

  SyncState copyWith({SyncStatus? status, DateTime? lastSyncedAt, String? errorMessage, int? pendingCount}) {
    return SyncState(
      status: status ?? this.status,
      lastSyncedAt: lastSyncedAt ?? this.lastSyncedAt,
      errorMessage: errorMessage,
      pendingCount: pendingCount ?? this.pendingCount,
    );
  }

  @override
  List<Object?> get props => [status, lastSyncedAt, errorMessage, pendingCount];
}

import 'package:flutter/material.dart';

/// Full color palette for one brightness mode. See design/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md
/// for the source-of-truth values — do not introduce new colors here without
/// updating that file first.
class AppColors {
  final Color background;
  final Color surface;
  final Color textPrimary;
  final Color textSecondary;
  final Color border;
  final Color success;
  final Color danger;
  final Color warning;

  /// Primary action fill (solid buttons, active nav item, active filter pill).
  /// Inverts between light/dark so it always reads as the highest-contrast
  /// "ink" color against [background].
  final Color primaryAction;

  /// Text/icon color to use on top of [primaryAction].
  final Color onPrimaryAction;

  const AppColors({
    required this.background,
    required this.surface,
    required this.textPrimary,
    required this.textSecondary,
    required this.border,
    required this.success,
    required this.danger,
    required this.warning,
    required this.primaryAction,
    required this.onPrimaryAction,
  });

  static const light = AppColors(
    background: Color(0xFFFAFAFA),
    surface: Color(0xFFFFFFFF),
    textPrimary: Color(0xFF0A0A0A),
    textSecondary: Color(0xFF6B6B6B),
    border: Color(0xFFE5E5E5),
    success: Color(0xFF16A34A),
    danger: Color(0xFFDC2626),
    warning: Color(0xFFD97706),
    primaryAction: Color(0xFF0A0A0A),
    onPrimaryAction: Color(0xFFFFFFFF),
  );

  static const dark = AppColors(
    background: Color(0xFF121212),
    surface: Color(0xFF1E1E1E),
    textPrimary: Color(0xFFF5F5F5),
    textSecondary: Color(0xFFA0A0A0),
    border: Color(0xFF2A2A2A),
    success: Color(0xFF16A34A),
    danger: Color(0xFFDC2626),
    warning: Color(0xFFD97706),
    primaryAction: Color(0xFFF5F5F5),
    onPrimaryAction: Color(0xFF0A0A0A),
  );
}

/// Convenience accessor: `context.colors.textPrimary`.
extension AppColorsX on BuildContext {
  AppColors get colors =>
      Theme.of(this).brightness == Brightness.dark ? AppColors.dark : AppColors.light;
}
